# Adversarial review: the simplified Dimensity 9300+ against Snapdragon 8 Gen 3 post

You are reviewing a technical blog post (not a paper) on a research site. Be critical and adversarial:
find what is wrong, unfair, overclaimed, missing, duplicated or still too dense. Do not praise.

## What the post is

A Galaxy Tab S10+ (Dimensity 9300+) and a Galaxy S24 Ultra (Snapdragon 8 Gen 3), measured the same way
on Firebase Device Streaming: for 9 models, every pairing of the processor that reads the prompt
(prefill) and the one that writes the reply (decode) over CPU, GPU and NPU; heat while each ran; and
heat probes (busy loops, then the same Gemma 3 1B turns cool against heated) to look for soft and hard
throttling.

## The owner's request for this revision (verbatim, abridged)

- "This is kinda dense. I want you to simplify." (the fastest table, the findings, the pairing table and
  its note, the heat-probe figure, the method rows, long bars)
- "can this layout be improved rather than scattered?" (the matrix view's four rows of switches)
- "Exclude the earlier or later duplicate/redundance/unecessary reruns."
- "Keep our branding and how we do it. But I don't like dense text. It's better to split it to bullets,
  tables, or multiple short sections. Keep it focused."
- "This is a technical blog post, not yet a research paper."
- Long horizontal bars stacked one per row were "terrible": "Stack em up with each other and they're
  unreadable." Lines that are long "to capture attention" dilute the meaning.
- Later: "all button should still be shown" (the All option in the matrix model picker).
- Later: "I don't want you to [exclude] key sections and visualizations. Don't hide anything necessary.
  Just limit the denseness. Work around it with the layout and our preference."

So the bar is: nothing key removed or hidden behind a collapsed fold, but nothing dense either.

## Standing rules

- No em dashes or en dashes anywhere (code comments included). "→" arrows and "×" are fine.
- Every number must come from the generated data; a headline must hold for the data it states.
- Fair comparison: compare only what both devices ran the same way; one-sided results labelled.
- Bounded claims: say what was measured, not what it implies beyond that.
- Excluded on purpose: the S24's earlier subset runs replaced by full-turn parity runs (`sdExtra`), the
  S24's cut-short first run (`sdDropped`), and the heat-probe sessions that were cut short or superseded
  (only each device's longest finished long probe is shown, plus the 20-minute probes). Two longer
  probe reruns (s24o, s14) are still running and will replace s24m and s12 when they finish.

## Files

- `site/page.tsx`: the post as it is now. `site/DuoViews.tsx`, `site/duo.tsx`, `site/Switches.tsx`,
  `site/pd-parts.tsx`, `site/Post.tsx`: its components.
- `site/prefill-decode-mtk-data.ts`: the generated data. `site/prefill-decode-mtk.ts`: helpers.
  `site/build-prefill-decode-mtk-data.mjs`: the generator.
- `site/old-page-before-rewrite.tsx`: the version before this revision (dense, but complete). Use it to
  check nothing key was lost.
- `site/reference-prefill-decode-page.tsx`: the site's earlier post in the same series, for house style.
- `notes/s24-NOTES.md`, `notes/tablet-NOTES.md`: the run notes.
- `rendered-default-views.txt`: the page's text as rendered with each switch at its default.
- Screenshots: t1 to t5 (desktop, 1280 px, top to bottom; taken just before a last small fix that
  moved "no matrix before" under the heat minutes) and m1 (phone, 390 px, middle of the page).

## What to check, in this order

1. Correctness: any number, label, headline or bullet the data does not support; any wrong denominator,
   wrong device, wrong model, a run that is excluded but still counted, or a computed sentence that
   would read wrong for some model. Check the memory, first-token and fastest tables against the data.
   Check "Qwen3 4B on the Snapdragon: its memory guard's margin was lowered to 768 MiB" against the S24
   notes: is that true of the run now shown (s24l) or only of the replaced one?
2. Fairness: anything that compares the two devices on different work, or wording that favours one.
3. Lost content: anything key in the old page that the new page dropped or buried (compare figure by
   figure). Name it and say where it should go without making the page dense again.
4. Density: any bullet over about 25 words, any table too wide or too tall for what it says, any place
   that is still a paragraph, any figure whose switches are confusing, any figure that should be split.
5. Duplication: the same fact stated in two places without need (findings vs figure bullets vs limits),
   or two views that show the same numbers.
6. House style: anything that breaks the reference post's conventions (findings, Figure, Rows, More),
   any dash, any jargon a reader would not know without a definition.

## Output

A numbered list, most severe first. For each: the file and line, what is wrong, why it matters, and the
exact fix (replacement text or code). Mark each CORRECTNESS, FAIRNESS, LOST, DENSITY, DUPLICATION or
STYLE. Then a short list of what you checked and found correct, so the owner knows what was covered.

   293	// One probe pair, cool → heated, in one of four readings. Rates carry a short bar on one scale per view:
   294	// the fill is the heated rate, the tick the cool one, so a gain reads longer than no change.
   295	type ProbeView = "decode" | "prefill" | "limits" | "step";
   296	const rateOf = (p: MtkProbe, view: ProbeView) => (view === "decode" ? p.decodeTokS : p.prefillTokS);
   297	const probeScale = (view: ProbeView) =>
   298	  Math.max(
   299	    1,

   301	      (["6", "2"] as const).flatMap((t) => (u.probes[`cool${t}`] && u.probes[`hot${t}`] ? [rateOf(u.probes[`hot${t}`], view) / rateOf(u.probes[`cool${t}`], view)] : [])),

   304	function ProbeCell({ c, h, view, d }: { c?: MtkProbe; h?: MtkProbe; view: ProbeView; d: "mtk" | "sd" }) {
   305	  if (!c || !h) return <span className="text-ink-soft">not run</span>;
   306	  if (view === "limits")
   307	    return (
   308	      <span className="block min-w-[16rem]">
   309	        <span className="block whitespace-nowrap">
   310	          status {c.status} → {h.status} {STATUS[h.status]}, AP {c.apC.toFixed(0)} → {h.apC.toFixed(0)} °C
   311	        </span>
   312	        <span className="block text-xs leading-5 text-ink-soft">
   313	          {h.ceilingsGhz.map((g, i, all) => (
   314	            <span key={i} className="mr-2 inline-block whitespace-nowrap">
   315	              {groupsOf(d)[i] ?? `group ${i + 1}`} {atTop(c, d) ? "" : `${c.ceilingsGhz[i].toFixed(2)} → `}
   316	              {g.toFixed(2)}
   317	              {i === all.length - 1 ? " GHz" : ""}
   318	            </span>
   319	          ))}
   320	        </span>
   321	      </span>
   322	    );
   323	  if (view === "step")
   324	    return (
   325	      <span className="whitespace-nowrap">
   326	        {grouped(c.longestStepMs)} → {grouped(h.longestStepMs)} ms
   327	      </span>
   328	    );
   329	  const a = rateOf(c, view);
   330	  const b = rateOf(h, view);
   331	  const f = b / a;
   332	  const scale = probeScale(view);
   333	  const change = Math.round(100 * (f - 1));
   334	  const show = (x: number) => (view === "decode" ? x.toFixed(1) : grouped(Math.round(x)));
   335	  return (
   336	    <span className="inline-flex items-center gap-2 whitespace-nowrap">
   337	      <span>
   338	        {show(a)} → {show(b)}{" "}
   339	        <span className="text-ink-soft">
   340	          ({change < 0 ? "−" : "+"}
   341	          {Math.abs(change)}%)
   342	        </span>
   343	      </span>
   344	      <span aria-hidden className="relative inline-block h-2 w-14 rounded-full bg-ink/5">
   345	        <span className="absolute inset-y-0 left-0 rounded-full" style={{ width: `${(100 * f) / scale}%`, backgroundColor: f < 1 ? CPU_FASTER : "var(--color-blue)" }} />
   346	        <span className="absolute -inset-y-0.5 w-px bg-ink" style={{ left: `${100 / scale}%` }} />
   347	      </span>
   348	    </span>
   349	  );
   350	}

import json, collections
from pathlib import Path
for name in ('results-s14hot.jsonl','results-s24ohot.jsonl'):
    rows = [(n,json.loads(s)) for n,s in enumerate(Path(name).read_text().splitlines(),1)]
    print(name)
    groups=collections.defaultdict(list)
    for n,r in rows:
        if r['kind']=='turn':
            groups[r['label']].append((n,r))

            print(n,{k:v for k,v in r.items() if k not in ('model','npu_dir','npu_devices')})
    for label,rr in groups.items():

/bin/zsh -lc "python3 -B - <<'PY'
import json, math, re, collections
from pathlib import Path
for stem in ('s14hot','s24ohot'):
    name=f'results-{stem}.jsonl'
    rows=[json.loads(s) for s in Path(name).read_text().splitlines()]
    groups=collections.defaultdict(list)
    for r in rows:
        if r['kind']=='turn': groups[r['label'].split('-')[-1]].append(r)
    rates={}
    for k,rs in groups.items():
        rates[k] = {'prefill':sum(r['new_tokens'] for r in rs)*1000/sum(r['prompt_ms'] for r in rs),'decode':sum(r['decode_steps'] for r in rs)*1000/sum(r['decode_ms'] for r in rs)}
        steps=[s for r in rs for s in r['step_ms']]
        print(stem,k,'rates', {k:round(v,6) for k,v in rates[k].items()}, 'step_max',max(steps),'>500',sum(s>500 for s in steps))
    for t in ('6','2'):
        print(stem, t,'changes%', {k:round(100*(rates[f'hot{t}'][k]/rates[f'cool{t}'][k]-1),6) for k in ('prefill','decode')})
    log=Path(f'hot-{stem}.log').read_text().splitlines()
    on=[int(s.split()[0]) for s in log if 'heat on' in s]
    off=[int(s.split()[0]) for s in log if 'heat off' in s]
    print('heat_minutes',[(b-a)/60 for a,b in zip(on,off)],'total_busy_minutes',sum(b-a for a,b in zip(on,off))/60,'whole_window_minutes',(int(log[-2].split()[0])-on[0])/60)
    reference=groups['cool6']
    print('all_turns_and_generated_tokens_identical',all([(r['conv'],r['turn'],r['pos'],r['new_tokens'],r['decode_steps'],r['tokens']) for r in rs]==[(r['conv'],r['turn'],r['pos'],r['new_tokens'],r['decode_steps'],r['tokens']) for rs in groups.values()]))
PY" in /private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-alpha-mobile-inference/4f6e0161-05e2-42cd-beac-ee96f21f3667/scratchpad/codex-simplify/round3

/bin/zsh -lc "node - <<'JS'
const fs = require('fs');
const source=fs.readFileSync('mtkHot.ts','utf8');
const all=Function("'`return ${source.slice(source.indexOf('"'}[] = [')+6)}"'`)();
const hot20=all.filter(u=>!u.long);
const longest=['"'mtk','sd'].flatMap(d=>all.filter(u=>u.device===d&&u.long).sort((a,b)=>b.heatMinutes-a.heatMinutes).slice(0,1));
const probes=[...hot20,...longest];
console.log('shown',probes.map(u=>"'`${u.device}/${u.session}/${u.unit}`));
console.log('"'softOnly',probes.every(u=>u.minOnline===8&&['6','2'].every(t=>u.probes["'`hot${t}`].ceilingsGhz.some((g,i)=>g<u.probes[`cool${t}`].ceilingsGhz[i]))));
console.log('"'noStall',probes.every(u=>Object.values(u.probes).every(p=>p.slowSteps===0)));
for (const d of ['mtk','sd']) {
 const loss=hot20.filter(u=>u.device===d).flatMap(u=>['6','2'].filter(t=>u.probes["'`hot${t}`].status>=4).map(t=>100*(1-u.probes[`hot${t}`].decodeTokS/u.probes[`cool${t}`].decodeTokS)));

#!/usr/bin/env node
// Generates lib/prefill-decode-mtk-data.ts from OpenWeights' raw MediaTek results
// (tools/mediatek/results/2026-11-16-mt6989/s*/results-*.jsonl), so the MediaTek prefill/decode
// page never retypes a number.
//
//   OPENWEIGHTS=../../mobile-inference node scripts/build-prefill-decode-mtk-data.mjs [ref|worktree]
//
// Each model's nine cells ran on one Galaxy Tab S10+ unit in one Device Streaming session, after a
// throwaway warm-up, each cell behind a cooling gate (AP and SKIN at or under 55 C, every CPU
// ceiling at its top, thermal status 0, at most 120 s). This tablet passed the gate before almost
// every cell, so unlike the Snapdragon page no cell is set aside for starting cool: all compared.
import { execFileSync } from "node:child_process";
import { readFileSync, writeFileSync } from "node:fs";
import { resolve } from "node:path";

/bin/zsh -lc "python3 -B - <<'PY'
import json,collections
from pathlib import Path
all_signatures=[]
for stem in ('s14hot','s24ohot'):
    rows=[json.loads(s) for s in Path(f'results-{stem}.jsonl').read_text().splitlines()]
    groups=collections.defaultdict(list)
    for r in rows:
        if r['kind']=='turn': groups[r['label'].split('-')[-1]].append(r)
    rates={}
    for k,rs in groups.items():
        rates[k]={'prefill':sum(r['new_tokens'] for r in rs)*1000/sum(r['prompt_ms'] for r in rs),'decode':sum(r['decode_steps'] for r in rs)*1000/sum(r['decode_ms'] for r in rs)}
        all_signatures.append([(r['conv'],r['turn'],r['pos'],r['new_tokens'],r['decode_steps'],r['tokens']) for r in rs])
        print(stem,k,'rates', {k:round(v,6) for k,v in rates[k].items()})
    for t in ('6','2'):
        print(stem,t,'changes%',{k:round(100*(rates[f'hot{t}'][k]/rates[f'cool{t}'][k]-1),6) for k in ('prefill','decode')})
    log=Path(f'hot-{stem}.log').read_text().splitlines()
    on=[int(s.split()[0]) for s in log if 'heat on' in s]
    off=[int(s.split()[0]) for s in log if 'heat off' in s]
    print(stem,'heat_minutes',[(b-a)/60 for a,b in zip(on,off)],'total_busy_minutes',sum(b-a for a,b in zip(on,off))/60)
print('identical_turns_and_output_tokens_across_all_eight',all(s==all_signatures[0] for s in all_signatures))

# Working in this repository

OpenWeights is a native Android app (Kotlin, Jetpack Compose) that runs open-weight LLMs
entirely on the phone: GGUF files through llama.cpp and compiled `.pte` exports through
ExecuTorch. No accounts, no telemetry, no cloud. It is on Google Play as
`io.github.alpharomercoma.openweights`, Apache-2.0, one maintainer. This file is the entry
point for any coding agent or contributor; it is short on purpose and links to the rest.

## Build and check

```sh
export JAVA_HOME=/opt/homebrew/opt/openjdk@21   # non-interactive shells find no JDK otherwise
./gradlew ktlintCheck detekt test jvmTest        # what CI runs, in this order
./gradlew :app:assembleDebug                     # debug APK, package io.github.alpharomercoma.openweights.debug
./gradlew :app:bundleRelease                     # signed Play bundle; needs keystore.properties and upload.jks
```

Never run two Gradle invocations at the same time. The second one waits on the first's
lock, both look hung, and a killed daemon leaves a stale lock behind. Start one, wait for it.
The pre-commit hook (`lefthook.yml`) runs ktlint on staged Kotlin only, without Gradle.

Setup, submodules, SDK and NDK versions: `CONTRIBUTING.md`. Module layout and how the two
engines sit behind one interface: `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`. Everything measured, decided or
learned, by date: `docs/CONTEXT.md` (long; search it, do not read it top to bottom) and the
index of research notes at `docs/research/README.md`.

## Rules that are not visible in the code

- **Secrets.** `upload.jks`, `keystore.properties` and `local.properties` exist in the
  working tree and are ignored; never stage them, never copy their contents anywhere. The
  Hugging Face token lives only in the shell environment as `HF_TOKEN`: never write it to a
  file, a commit, a log or a note. A hook denies commands that try (`tools/hooks/guard.py`).
- **Measure, do not assume.** Every default in this codebase is justified by a number from a
  real phone, and the KDoc says which. A change meant to make inference faster comes with
  before and after figures from a device, written up under `docs/research/`.
- **Prompt bytes are cache keys.** The prompt head must be byte-identical between turns, or
  a hybrid model pays a full re-prefill measured at eleven to nineteen seconds. Anything that
  varies per turn goes at the tail, never the head. See `docs/research/date-in-the-prompt.md`.
- **Module boundaries.** `:core:*` never imports from `:app`. If a change needs that, the
  code is in the wrong module.
- **Comments say why, not what.** A comment explains the constraint the next line obeys.
- **Prose style.** No em dashes or en dashes anywhere: commit messages, docs, comments,
  strings, this file. Recast the sentence with a comma, a colon or a full stop.
- **Egress is named and switchable.** Model downloads go to Hugging Face. The assistant's
  own web tools are the only other thing that leaves the device, each behind its own switch.
  Do not add a network call anywhere else.

## Git

- Commit subjects follow the log: a scope, a colon, and a sentence that says what changed
  and why (`engine: a .pte the runner would refuse is refused before it opens, because
  ...`). The body says what was measured or found. Agents append their own attribution
  trailer.
- Push with the GitHub CLI's credentials on this machine:
  `git -c credential.helper='!gh auth git-credential' push https://github.com/ExperimentalMachines/openweights.git main`
- The version code is the commit count on `main`. Never rewrite published history; a
  version code that goes down cannot be uploaded. Force pushes to `main` are denied by hook.
- CI (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`) runs lint, unit tests and a debug assembly on every push.
  A commit is not done until `gh run list --branch main --limit 1` says `completed success`.

## The test phone

The primary device is a POCO X8 Pro Max (MediaTek MT6991, Android 16, HyperOS) on wireless
debugging; its port changes and its ROM blocks plain `adb install`. The exact procedure,
from finding the phone over mDNS to running an instrumented test, is the `phone-deploy`
skill under `.claude/skills/`. It is a Markdown file; any agent can read it.

Screen-off measurements on this phone are throttled by the ROM and vary wildly. Wake,
unlock and cool the phone before timing anything.

## What agents should not do

- Do not add telemetry, analytics, crash reporting or an account of any kind.
- Do not gate a feature on a SoC name; read the runtime capability instead.
- Do not "fix" a default without the measurement that set it; find the KDoc first.
- Do not run the instrumented tests through `connectedAndroidTest` on the phone; it cannot
  install. Use the skill.

from pathlib import Path
from datetime import datetime,timezone
for dev,session,logname in [('mediatek','s14','mtklog-s14.txt'),('snapdragon','s24o','sdlog-s24o.txt')]:
    root=Path('/Users/alpha/mobile-inference/tools')/dev/'results'/('2026-11-16-mt6989' if dev=='mediatek' else '2026-11-16-s24')/session
    heat=(root/f'hot-{session}hot.log').read_text().splitlines()
    start=int(next(s for s in heat if 'heat on' in s).split()[0])
    end=int(next(s for s in heat if 'probe hot2' in s).split()[0])
    samples=[]
    statuses=[]
    for n,line in enumerate((root/logname).read_text().splitlines(),1):
        if not line or not line[0].isdigit(): continue
        left,sep,right=line.partition('|')
        cols=left.split()
        if start<=int(cols[0])<=end:
            samples.append((n,cols))
            if sep: statuses.append((n, int(cols[0]),[float(v) for v in right.split()]))
    print(session,'samples',len(samples),'lines',[samples[0][0],samples[-1][0]], 'online_values',sorted(set(c[1] for n,c in samples)), 'isolated_values',sorted(set(c[2] for n,c in samples)))
    for i,kind in enumerate(('status','AP','SKIN')):
        peak=max(s[2][i] for s in statuses)
        first=next(s for s in statuses if s[2][i]==peak)
        print(kind,'peak',peak,'first_at_line',first[0],'minutes_after_heat_on',(first[1]-start)/60)
    print('heat_on_utc',datetime.fromtimestamp(start,timezone.utc).isoformat(),'final_probe_done_utc',datetime.fromtimestamp(end,timezone.utc).isoformat())
    session_lines=(root/'session.log').read_text().splitlines()
    for n,s in enumerate(session_lines,1):
        if any(k in s for k in ('HOT_DONE','21:23','21:27','18:33','cleanup','cancel','ended','pulled','download','finished')):
            print('session.log',n,s[:400])
